### TASK 3: CREATIVE ENGINE REPOSITORY GLOSSARY


* **Plate Isolation ("Clean Your Plate")**: A high-priority visual effects optimization step where background images are completely cleared of artifacts, text blocks, and dynamic objects to maximize spatial accuracy before computing video motion vectors [0:12:19].
* **Color Transfer Mapping**: An algorithmic project pass within Higgsfield AI that extracts the visual lookup profile of a target frame and locks it onto subsequent scene generations, ensuring grading harmony across various sequences [0:08:59].
* **Position-Lock Keyframing**: Using targeted screenshots extracted from stable video renders as layout anchors, preventing structural features (like steering wheel spokes or dashboards) from shifting between distinct cuts [0:16:02, 0:17:38].
* **Model Over-Correction**: A prompting logic trap where refining character behaviors (e.g., filtering out an exaggerated smirk) causes the model to suppress all emotional warmth, turning the actor into a stiff performance layout [0:25:05, 0:25:16].
* **Intersection Frame-Out Rule**: An operational editing choice where complex traffic elements, tracking lines, or shifting signals are framed completely out of camera bounds to prevent diffusion cores from rendering conflicting legal logic (e.g., simultaneous red and green lights) [0:20:38].
* **FPV Flyover Distortion**: An artifact anomaly where extreme wide aerial captures degrade vehicle physics, causing background traffic models to drift across solid lane partitions or randomly warp spatial velocity scales [0:20:46, 0:22:39].
* **Anatomical Drift Mismatch**: A face tracking error caused by unedited, multi-view asset sheets, where competing full-body character silhouettes break mid-point generation paths and alter actor features across continuous shots [0:08:22].

### TASK 2: ARCHITECTURAL BLUEPRINT LAYER


#### 1. PRIMARY USE CASE
*   **Target Industries / Content Types**: Commercial Ads, Cinematic Movie Trailers, Music Videos, Multicam Narrative Dialogue, High-End VFX Previsualization, Action & Automotive Prompts.


#### 2. KEY ASSET LINKS
*   **Higgsfield Prompts & Master Files**: `https://higgsfield.ai`
*   **Blender Plugin Installer**: `https://higgsfield.ai`
*   **Hixel/Higgsfield Discord**: `https://discord.gg`


#### 3. TECHNIQUE & WORKFLOW MAPPING
The baseline production standard relies on **3D Previsualization (Previs) Tracking** inside Blender to enforce structural logic, which is then translated by a foundational LLM into frame-accurate prompts executed by an AI spatial video engine (Seedance 2.5).




[Claude LLM] --------(Hixel Bridge Connector Link)---------> [Blender 3D Viewport]
| |
(Generates Layout) (Outputs Gray Box Render)
| |
v v
[Asset References] ---------------------------------------------> [Seedance 2.5 Engine]
|
(Final Stable Video)


*   **Phase 1: Synchronization & Linking Architecture**
    *   Initialize the Hixel/Higgsfield application to retrieve a unique **Bridge URL**.
    *   In the LLM interface (Claude), access connectors and paste the Bridge URL.
    *   Install and drag the Hixel python plugin into the Blender environment, logging in to activate the local viewport toolbar.
*   **Phase 2: LLM-Driven 3D Blockout Creation**
    *   Configure Claude to **Co-work Mode** and switch the asset builder engine (**Fable 5**) parameter selection to **High**.
    *   Input conversational landscape layouts to Claude; the bridge processes rules to generate physical meshes, targets, rails, and camera keyframes inside Blender.
*   **Phase 3: Refinement & Kinetic Tweaks**
    *   Iterate on architectural details (e.g., surface textures, framing variety, lens animations) using programmatic textual prompts directly through the chat layer.
    *   Manually clean up trajectories inside Blender by directly transforming structural path control points without needing to regenerate prompts.
    *   Introduce procedural camera weight by adding subtle handheld noise offsets to camera constraints or target objects.
*   **Phase 4: Gray Box Pre-Render Export**
    *   Set output resolution to **1920x1080 (16:9 standard)**, framerate to **24fps**, container/codec to **MP4 (MPEG-4)**.
    *   Trigger `View Menu -> Viewport Render Animation` to export a clean gray box guide video.
*   **Phase 5: Time-Synced AI Translation**
    *   Upload the reference gray box animation along with specific character concept sheets/style imagery back to Claude.
    *   Command the LLM to analyze the movement parameters and output a precise, timestamped script for generation.
    *   Paste the generated second-by-second prompt block into the Higgsfield generation canvas, set the temporal baseline to match the blockout clip duration, select **Seedance 2.5**, and render.


#### 4. EXACT GENERATIVE PROMPTS
Every token string and configuration change demonstrated across the five master layouts:


*   **Workflow Technique A: Continuous Cinematic One-Take**
    *   *Prompt 1 (Initial Generation)*: `"a corridor a person walking down it camera following behind"`
    *   *Prompt 2 (Detail Enhancement)*: `"now make it more interesting add texture to the walls use different framings and animate the lens"`
    *   *Prompt 3 (AI Video Engine Execution)*: `"write a 30-second cense prompt based on this video blocking read the input video and write out the prompt second by second to match the camera moves in the clip"`
*   **Workflow Technique B: Multi-Person Dialogue Rig (180° Rule Preserved)**
    *   *Prompt 1 (Geometry Setup)*: `"a new project one round table six people around it no faces or bodies yet"`
    *   *Prompt 2 (Cinematic Framing)*: Input exact cinematic shot list defining rails, spatial anchors, tracking targets, and **four distinct camera cuts**.
    *   *Prompt 3 (Kinetic Movement)*: `"some more natural handheld movement i want to keep it subtle"`
*   **Workflow Technique C: Viral Kinetic Trajectories**
    *   *Orbit Setup*: Prompt detailing a **continuous single-shot orbital rotation** that orbits a multi-person street crew clustered by a vehicle, tracks a basketball athlete mid-motion, and settles directly onto a central hero asset while background assets drift.
    *   *Floor Rise Architecture*: Prompt demanding a vertical, **continuous Z-axis camera rocket trajectory** passing cleanly through multiple ceiling panels of a building, revealing distinct asset scenarios on each tier.
    *   *Robo-Arm Mimicry*: Prompt implementing custom interpolation paths featuring strict **control point offsets** where the viewport tracks an independent coordinate box rather than the target's origin.
*   **Workflow Technique D: Hypermotion Commercial Asset Mod**
    *   *Prompt 1 (Client Revision Layout)*: Comprehensive programmatic breakdown mapping out editing revisions frame-by-frame, tracking canister transformations, vector fields for flying debris, while intentionally leaving fluid dynamics blank via `"keep it black in the blockout"` to delegate liquid simulation logic to the generator.
    *   *Prompt 2 (Speed Ramping Injection)*: Dynamic adjustment configuration to modify camera velocity arrays: `"the camera slams in pauses for a beat and accelerates into the next cut"`.
*   **Workflow Technique E: 19-Shot Multi-Genre Multiplier**
    *   *Prompt Design*: Structural configuration mapping **19 unique multi-camera coordinates across a 30-second timeline**, processing two digital characters, an automotive asset, and location geometry data.
    *   *Aesthetic Layer Alternation (Non-textual configurations swapping style tokens while maintaining structural logs)*:
        *   **Style Toggle 1**: `2.5D Style` (Hybrid 2D/3D illustrative rendering mechanics).
        *   **Style Toggle 2**: `Full 2D Ink on Paper Style` (Traditional hand-drawn cell animation translation).
        *   **Style Toggle 3**: `3D Toy Box Style` (Miniature plastic-shelled character optimization model).


---


### TASK 3: CREATIVE ENGINE REPOSITORY GLOSSARY


*   **Previs (Previsualization)**: The practice of designing and blocking out scenes using raw 3D geometry, camera trajectories, and structural timing before executing full renders or burning processing credits.
*   **Co-Work Mode**: An operational setting within the Hixel-Claude bridge that optimizes background tools, allowing the LLM to efficiently generate and manipulate 3D scripts for Blender.
*   **Fable 5**: A high-efficiency AI model module used to generate and handle detailed, editable 3D objects within connected pipelines.
*   **Pixel/Hixel Bridge**: A synchronized data pipeline that connects a text-based LLM interface to a local 3D application viewport, converting text commands into editable 3D assets and keyframes.
*   **Viewport Render (Playblast)**: A rapid pre-rendering technique that captures real-time viewport motion graphics, exporting quick reference clips without processing complex lighting, raytracing, or volumetric effects.
*   **Seedance 2.5**: A spatial video generation model designed to read gray box video tracking data and build consistent final visuals around it.
*   **180° Rule**: A foundational cinematography standard that keeps the camera on one side of an imaginary axis between characters to maintain consistent eye-lines and spatial orientation across edits.
*   **Slop / Total Slop**: Algorithmic artifacts or unintended camera movement (like spinning 180 degrees or sudden perspective warping) that happen when video generation models rely entirely on text without 3D spatial guidelines.
*   **Character Sheet Staging**: Injecting descriptive multi-angle character reference illustrations into the visual pipeline to lock in uniform clothing, facial structures, and textures across multiple cuts.
*   **Target Offset Tracking**: Separating camera direction logic from a character's coordinate origin by binding the camera constraint to an empty target item, creating more natural, lifelike camera framing.
*   **Hypermotion**: A commercial visual style featuring explosive asset separations, extreme slow-motion freezing, macro lens tracking, and precise time manipulation.
*   **Speed Ramp**: An editing technique that alters clip velocity—speeding up or slowing down footage within a single shot—to create dynamic, hard-hitting transitions without breaking visual continuity.
*   **Structural Log (vs. Style Layer)**: Splitting a prompt canvas into two distinct workflows: a baseline guide that locks in timing, choreography, and camera paths, and an aesthetic overlay that controls textures, lighting, and artistic styling.

* Which **specific use case template** (e.g., Dialogue Rig or Automotive Commercial) you want to automate next?
* If you need **custom Python scripts** to automate the scene cleanup process inside Blender.
* If you want to build a automated **prompt framework library** for Claude's Co-Work mode.
</FollowUp>

### TASK 2: ARCHITECTURAL BLUEPRINT LAYER


#### 1. PRIMARY USE CASE
*   **Target Industries / Content Types**: High-Concept Indie Film VFX, Television Commercials (TVCs), Spatial Music Videos, Infinite Loop Social Media Content, Interactive Browser-Based Previs Planning [01:148].


#### 2. KEY ASSET LINKS
*   **Astra Skills & Higgsfield Prompts**: `https://higgsfield.ai` [01:148]
*   **Blender Plugin Installer**: `https://higgsfield.ai` [01:148]
*   **Higgsfield Discord Portal**: `https://discord.gg` [01:148]


#### 3. TECHNIQUE & WORKFLOW MAPPING
The generation standard upgrades the production chain by executing **Model Context Protocol (MCP)** tool-use [01:148, 01:184], enabling an advanced LLM (GPT Astra 6) to directly manipulate native desktop software (Blender) via computer-vision loops [01:147, 01:164], or spin up light web scenes using an integrated browser environment (3D Jutsu) [01:148, 01:209].




[GPT Astra 6 Chat Interface]
/
(MCP Computer Use) (Web Engine integration)
/
v v
[Blender 3D Desktop] [3D Jutsu Browser Engine]
\ /
\ /
v v
[Higgsfield MCP / Seedance 2.5 Video Node]


*   **Phase 1: Local & Multi-Agent Connectivity**
    *   Deploy the Higgsfield plugin architecture straight into the Blender client script engine [01:159].
    *   Open the conversational developer console, navigate to the plugins panel, select Higgsfield, and initiate cross-app handshakes via the terminal command [01:160, 01:161]: `add Higgsfield/ use Blender` [01:161].
*   **Phase 2: Autonomous MCP Mesh & Camera Blocking**
    *   Provide story outlines or geometric constraints directly inside the conversational thread [01:162].
    *   Astra activates custom **Computer Use skills**, using optical-loop validation to view the active Blender viewport [01:164]. It autonomously generates 3D blockout shapes, timeline parameters, spatial tracking arrays, and camera path splines [01:164, 01:165, 01:201].
*   **Phase 3: Browser-Based Scene Extrapolation (Alternative Path)**
    *   When utilizing the standalone **3D Jutsu web interface**, users can select Astra to quickly draft fluid node routes directly in the browser environment without requiring desktop hardware [01:209, 01:214].
    *   Assign structural color-coded tracking signatures to control individual actors and objects (e.g., *Black mesh* = Male lead, *White mesh* = Female lead, *Yellow mesh* = Workers) [01:222].
    *   Draw independent vector lines for camera paths vs. character trajectories to refine physical movement fields [01:215, 01:223].
*   **Phase 4: Multi-Model Asset Synthesis Pipeline**
    *   Isolate individual keyframe frames from the raw 3D blockout sequence [01:179].
    *   Send layout images to specialized image generation nodes (**Nana Banana**) to establish locked set designs and environment baselines [01:179, 01:180].
    *   Simultaneously generate tailored multi-angle **Character Sheets** using a uniform seed image to lock down asset clothing and design [01:203, 01:224, 01:242].
*   **Phase 5: High-Fidelity Rendering & Token Injection**
    *   Consolidate all layout references, gray box animation guide tracks, character maps, and environmental reference plates [01:226, 01:244].
    *   Submit the complete generation packet using Higgsfield MCP running **Seedance 2.5** [01:148, 01:184]. Inject direct audio, choreography guidelines, and sound directions into a single prompt layer to output time-locked audio-visual sequences [01:240, 01:245, 01:246].


#### 4. EXACT GENERATIVE PROMPTS
Every user command, structural iteration, and configuration tweak mapped by technique:


*   **Workflow Technique A: Seamless Infinite Loop Mapping**
    *   *Prompt 1 (Initial Setup)*: Conversational prompt requesting an editable tracking spline featuring procedural handheld camera wobble [01:162].
    *   *Prompt 2 (Recursive Tweak)*: `"keep the room and the opening camera move then replace the ending with a pull back into a larger copy"` [01:172, 01:173].
    *   *Prompt 3 (Environment Pass via Nana Banana Interface)*: Command selecting the opening blockout frame as a layout master reference: `"old mansion study"` [01:179, 01:180].
    *   *Prompt 4 (Final Video Assembly)*: `Seedance 2.5 Engine, 30 Seconds, 16:9 Aspect Ratio, "one continuous shot"` [01:184, 01:185].
*   **Workflow Technique B: Impossible Mirror-Pass Rig**
    *   *Prompt 1 (Geometry Generation)*: Request asking Astra to construct a 15-second blockout environment featuring a mathematically identical, flipped room setup with dual automated characters moving in perfect sync [01:196, 01:197, 01:198]. The physical mirror asset must remain open geometry to let the viewport camera fly straight through without clipping [01:198].
    *   *Prompt 2 (Character Generation)*: `"detective character sheets"` [01:203].
*   **Workflow Technique C: Web-Based Browser Multi-Room Sequence (3D Jutsu)**
    *   *Prompt 1 (Initial Node Path Layout)*: `"one continuous 30-second shot following the couple from the street down the stairs through the corridor and kitchen and into the casino"` [01:214, 01:215]. Request separate camera and character paths [01:215].
    *   *Prompt 2 (Kinetic Polish & Hooks)*: Conversational request adding ceilings, access doors, and an opening visual hook: `"start behind the car's window the car pulls away when the camera follows the couple inside"` [01:218, 01:219, 01:220].
    *   *Prompt 3 (Style Layer Injection)*: `"late '70s casino"`. Text prompt manually inputs descriptions for secondary background characters: `"the cooks the staff and the guests"` [01:224, 01:225].
*   **Workflow Technique D: Crowd Formation & Audio Synchronization**
    *   *Prompt 1 (Structural Previs Request)*: Request asking Astra to generate a pre-edited 30-second multicam timeline array in Blender, explicitly pre-cutting close-up shots, wide tracking angles, top-down overhead maps, slow dolly moves, and quick push-ins [01:234, 01:235].
    *   *Prompt 2 (Mesh Alignment Setup)*: Spatial parameters setting a single central object geometry surrounded by 3 distinct target concentric rings and 10 absolute column arrays, locking in an asset distribution of exactly 10 in the inner track, 20 in the mid-track, and 36 on the exterior track [01:236, 01:237, 01:238].
    *   *Prompt 3 (Audio-Visual Multimodal Generation Request)*: Composite command running the raw spatial geometry, style plates, character models, fluid choreography constraints, and immediate **music and voice directions** simultaneously inside the prompt layout [01:244, 01:245, 01:246].
*   **Workflow Technique E: Variable Focal Length Commercials**
    *   *Prompt 1 (Scene Mechanics Request)*: Layout instructions for a 30-second multi-room pursuit scenario: A 10cm macro lead fled from an incoming cleaning appliance, got sucked straight through an open vacuum hose conduit node, settled inside a clear structural collection bin, followed by a wide pullback passing cleanly through a transparent material wall to present the final commercial product packaging [01:250, 01:254, 01:255, 01:256].
    *   *Prompt 2 (UI Dressing / Variable Camera Settings)*: Directing Astra to dynamically animate camera crop values, surface fields, and focal lens lengths on the fly to simulate high-end macro lens properties [01:252, 01:253].
    *   *Prompt 3 (Asset Wardrobe Specification)*: `"striped pajamas and slippers"` [01:260].

### TASK 3: CREATIVE ENGINE REPOSITORY GLOSSARY


*   **GPT Astra 6**: A multimodal LLM agent featuring autonomous tool-use skills, enabling it to write code, design layout blocks, and manipulate local software settings via desktop connectors [01:147].
*   **Model Context Protocol (MCP)**: An open standard connectivity framework that links an AI model directly to external developer environments, APIs, local file trees, and software tools [01:148, 01:184].
*   **Computer Use Skills**: The ability of an AI agent to see a live system UI screen, process layout coordinates, track pixel shifts, and interact with software tools just like a human designer [01:164].
*   **3D Jutsu**: A lightweight, web-based 3D workspace where users can generate, adjust, and map out scene blockouts inside a standard web browser before rendering [01:148, 01:209].
*   **Target Tracking Spine**: A structural vector curve inside a 3D canvas that tells a viewport camera exactly how to move and pivot around an asset smoothly [01:268].
*   **Nested Miniature (Droste Effect)**: A visual mapping technique where a complete scene environment is recursively cloned and placed inside a smaller object within itself, creating an infinite, looping transition [01:169, 01:170].
*   **Nana Banana**: A generative layout model used to convert simple gray box structural meshes into highly detailed, cinematic environment concept plates [01:179, 01:180].
*   **Impossible Mirror Shot**: A technical filming trick where a camera tracking spline passes cleanly through a faux mirror plane without revealing real equipment or camera crew reflections [01:193]. This is achieved by creating duplicate, inverted geometry paths on the other side of an open door frame [01:197, 01:198].
*   **Color-Coded Role Structuring**: Assigning high-contrast primary colors to distinct character groups inside a 3D blockout workspace to help multi-agent systems easily read and identify individual tracking roles [01:222].
*   **Concentric Crowd Clustering**: Arranging background actors or assets in structured concentric rings around a central target object to control density and spacing across multiple camera angles [01:236, 01:237].
*   **Multimodal Audio-Video Co-Generation**: A rendering method where text descriptions, motion guides, and audio tracks are processed in a single generation step, matching video pacing with real-time sound design [01:246].
*   **Macro Lens Simulation**: Emulating specialized close-up camera properties by heavily adjusting focal length, compression ratios, and depth-of-field settings to make full-scale assets look incredibly small [01:252, 01:253].
